# Skills · K · part 8

830 entries, kapitan-omegaconf-resolvers to karpenter-debug. [Index](README.md) · [Everything](../README.md)

- **kapitan-omegaconf-resolvers**: Use when making inventory blocks reusable with custom omegaconf resolvers: an inventory/resolvers.py that pass_resolvers() registers, expanding one compact object into the full kapitan.compile and kapitan.dependencies entries. Reach for this whenever the user wants a duplicatable block for helm, kustomize, or any input, uses ${merge:...}/${escape:...}, or asks how to stop copy-pasting the same compile/dependency boilerplate per component. ([Moep90/agent-toolkit-for-kapitan](https://github.com/Moep90/agent-toolkit-for-kapitan/tree/HEAD/plugins/kapitan-generators/skills/kapitan-omegaconf-resolvers) · 2 copies)
- **kapitan-project-scaffolding**: Use when creating a new Kapitan project or understanding an existing one's layout: inventory/targets and inventory/classes structure, the kapitan-reference layout, cookiecutter or cruft bootstrap, the system/ libraries, and the ./kapitan docker wrapper pattern. Reach for this whenever the user starts a Kapitan repo from scratch, asks where a file should go, or wonders why "kapitan" on their PATH is a shell script. ([Moep90/agent-toolkit-for-kapitan](https://github.com/Moep90/agent-toolkit-for-kapitan/tree/HEAD/plugins/kapitan-generators/skills/kapitan-project-scaffolding) · 2 copies)
- **kapitan-secrets-refs**: Use whenever a Kapitan project involves secrets, refs, or the ?{...} tag syntax: gpg, awskms, gcpkms, azkv, vaultkv, base64, plain refs, creating ref placeholders, or Tesoro. Reach for this when the user asks to add a secret, store a password, reference a key, or when you see ?{gpg:...} style tokens in inventory or templates. Explains ref syntax per backend and the rule that ref values are never revealed by an agent. ([Moep90/agent-toolkit-for-kapitan](https://github.com/Moep90/agent-toolkit-for-kapitan/tree/HEAD/plugins/kapitan-core/skills/kapitan-secrets-refs) · 2 copies)
- **kapitan-terraform-generator**: Use when generating Terraform through the kapicorp Terraform generator: a Kapitan inventory that produces provider blocks, resources, and project layout for Terraform rather than hand-written .tf files. Reach for this whenever the user configures Terraform via a Kapitan components or terraform block, sets a provider, or asks how to manage cloud infrastructure from the inventory. ([Moep90/agent-toolkit-for-kapitan](https://github.com/Moep90/agent-toolkit-for-kapitan/tree/HEAD/plugins/kapitan-generators/skills/kapitan-terraform-generator) · 2 copies)
- **kapitan-writing-kadet**: Use when writing or debugging a Kapitan kadet input: Python components that build manifests with BaseObj or BaseModel, read the inventory via inventory(), and return a Dict of output files. - **kaplan-meier**: Publication-ready Kaplan-Meier survival curves w/ log-rank test, number-at-risk table, Excel summary; 2+ groups. Trigger: Kaplan-Meier, KM curve, survival curve, survival analysis, log-rank test, survival plot, time-to-event plot, event-free survival, overall survival curve, KM plot, draw survival curve, 存活曲線, KM分析, 存活分析, 生存曲線, KM圖, 存活率, 對數秩檢定, 畫存活曲線, 做KM, 畫KM圖, 幫我畫KM curve, draw survival curves, 做存活分析, 畫存活曲線. - **kaplan-meier-cohort-survival-estimation**: Use when DeepCC subtype labels exist for validation cohorts (TANSBIG, UNT, UPP, NKI) with clinical metadata containing disease-free survival outcomes, and you need to show the classification has prognostic value. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/kaplan-meier-cohort-survival-estimation))
- **kaplan-meier-curve-visualization**: Use when metabolomics samples have survival times and event indicators and you need to visually validate risk-group stratification from a fitted Cox-PH or Cox-nnet model on pathway deregulation score (PDS) features. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/kaplan-meier-curve-visualization))
- **kaplan-meier-logrank-testing**: Use when TCGA bulk tumor samples have RSEM-normalized expression for a gene signature (e.g., the 73 TMGs) plus matched clinical OS/DSS time-status data, and you must test if high vs low signature expression separates survival. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/kaplan-meier-logrank-testing))
- **kaplan-meier-survival-analysis**: Use when single-modality (RNA-only or protein-only) program-usage stratification of CPTAC GBM patients shows no survival differences and a combined-modality classical+mesenchymal co-usage stratification is needed. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics/tree/HEAD/collections/multi-omics/v1/leaves/kaplan-meier-survival-analysis))
- **kaplan-meier-survival-analysis**: Use when a tumor cell subcluster (e.g., Louvain Cluster 4 from scanpyobj.h5ad) has cluster-specific DEGs and you need to test whether its gene signature stratifies TCGA patient cohorts (e.g., TCGA-LIHC) into high- vs low-survival groups. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/kaplan-meier-survival-analysis--7f898f31fe8eb9e3))
- **kaplan-meier-survival-analysis**: Use when patient samples have been partitioned into subgroups (e.g., from METAFlux flux-profile clustering of TCGA LUAD/LUSC) and you must test whether group membership predicts time-to-event survival outcomes. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/kaplan-meier-survival-analysis--ea10df6c516abe03))
- **kaplan-meier-survival-analysis**: Use when TCGA clinical data (survival time, status) has been merged with a continuous sample-level factor such as GSVA scores, and you need to test whether high vs low factor levels associate with poorer survival probability. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/kaplan-meier-survival-analysis--30ed16cc911c8ba9))
- **kaplan-meier-survival-analysis**: Use when a per-sample grouping (subtype label or risk score) must be validated against patient overall survival, requiring KM curves plus a log-rank p-value as the success criterion. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/kaplan-meier-survival-analysis--d992c060b0862360))
- **kaplan-meier-survival-analysis**: Use when a cell-type or gene signature identified in single-cell data must be validated for association with overall survival and radiologic response in independent bulk RNA-seq cohorts with clinical metadata. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/kaplan-meier-survival-analysis--c54decb6ff8f74c2))
- **kaplan-meier-survival-curve-analysis**: Use when per-sample viral or HERV presence calls (from viRNAtrap contigs) must be tested for association with TCGA overall survival by Kaplan-Meier curves and log-rank tests. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/kaplan-meier-survival-curve-analysis))
- **kaplan-meier-survival-curve-estimation**: Use when you have cancer patient clinical survival data (time, vital status) plus gene expression (e.g., TCGA expression for an eccDNA gene like EGFR) and need to test whether expression level stratification predicts prognosis. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/kaplan-meier-survival-curve-estimation))
- **kaplan-meier-survival-curve-estimation**: Use when the input is a survival table with sample IDs, survival times, censoring status, and group labels (like surv_example.csv) and the goal is a publication-ready survival curve figure per group. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/kaplan-meier-survival-curve-estimation))
- **kaplan-meier-survival-curve-estimation**: Use when patient samples have been partitioned into groups (e.g., network-based clusters) and merged with clinical metadata (survival time, censoring status), and you must test whether one group shows significantly worse survival (p &lt; 0.05). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics/tree/HEAD/collections/multi-omics/v1/leaves/kaplan-meier-survival-curve-estimation))
- **kaplan-meier-survival-curve-estimation**: Use when you have overall survival time/status for bulk tumor samples plus a per-sample numeric score (e.g., the 54th Miscell latent feature) and covariates Age, Sex, and TNM stage, and you need to test whether the score stratifies prognosis. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/kaplan-meier-survival-curve-estimation))
- **kaplan-meier-survival-estimation**: Use when a per-sample continuous score (e.g., ssGSEA enrichment of a gene signature in TCGA bulk RNA-seq) must be validated as a prognostic indicator against patient overall-survival metadata. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/kaplan-meier-survival-estimation))
- **kaplan-meier-survival-stratification**: Use when patient-level metadata includes overall survival time/event and predicted risk groups (e.g. VAE embeddings or IMDC classes) and you must test whether groups separate by OS via KM curves and log-rank p-values. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/kaplan-meier-survival-stratification))
- **kaplan-meier-survival-stratification**: Use when a per-patient prognostic risk score (e.g., TimiRS from LASSO Cox-selected marker pairs) must be validated by showing overall survival separation between high- and low-risk groups in one or more cohorts with survival endpoints. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/kaplan-meier-survival-stratification))

- **karpathy-graph-extract**: Stage 1 of the karpathy-graph pipeline. Use when the user asks to extract entities, pull relations out of documents, map a codebase into a graph, or when building a knowledge graph and no nodes exist yet. - **karpathy-graph-query**: Stage 4 of the karpathy-graph pipeline. Answer multi-hop questions from a knowledge graph by retrieving a bounded subgraph with edge citations, and ground or refute claims against actual graph paths. Use when the user asks a question that spans several facts, asks how two things connect, asks to verify or ground a claim, or when an agent needs task-specific context from an existing graph. - **karpathy-graph-resolve**: Stage 2 of the karpathy-graph pipeline. Collapse duplicate surface forms into canonical entities using context as evidence, reversibly and with a recorded rationale. Use after extraction when the graph contains aliases, abbreviations, or spelling variants of the same thing, when isolated-node count is high, or when the user asks to deduplicate or merge entities. - **karpathy-graph-swarm**: Fan the karpathy-graph pipeline out across parallel agents that share the graph as memory instead of copying transcripts. - **karpathy-guidelines**: Behavioral guidelines for cautious, minimal, and verifiable code changes. Triggers: The user explicitly requests this skill's application, for example by using `$karpathy-guidelines`, saying "apply karpathy-guidelines", or asking to use Karpathy Guidelines.
